<tr><td>175</td><td>Imazaquin<br>CAS No 81335-37-7<br>CIPAC No 699</td><td>2-[(RS)-4-isopropyl-4-methyl-5-oxo-2-imidazolin-2-yl]quinoline-3-carboxylic acid</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg (racemic mixture)</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2018</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as plant growth regulator may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on imazaquin, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 27 September 2011 shall be taken into account<br>The notifier shall submit confirmatory information as regards:<br>((a)) further data on the specification of the technical material;<br>((b)) the possible impact of the variable isomer-ratio in the technical material and of the preferential degradation and/or conversion of the mixture of isomers on the worker risk assessment, the consumer risk assessment and on the environment.<br>The notifier shall submit to the Member States, the Commission and the Authority the information set out in point (a) by 31 May 2012 and the information set out in point (b) within 2 years from the adoption of specific guidance.</td></tr>
<tr><td>176</td><td>Lenacil<br>CAS No 2164-08-1<br>CIPAC No 163</td><td>3-cyclohexyl-1,5,6,7-tetrahydrocyclopentapyrimidine-2,4(3H)-dione</td><td>≥ 975 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on lenacil, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 May 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the risk to aquatic organisms, especially algae and aquatic plants. Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, such as bufferzones between treated areas and surface water bodies;<br>the protection of the groundwater, where the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures and monitoring programmes shall be initiated to verify potential groundwater contamination from the metabolites IN-KF 313, M1, M2 and M3 in vulnerable zones, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission confirmatory information on the identity and characterisation of soil metabolites Polar B and Polars and metabolites M1, M2 and M3 whichoccurred in lysimeter studies and confirmatory data on rotational crops, including possible phytotoxic effects. They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 30 June 2012.<br>If a decision on the classification of lenacil under Regulation (EC) No 1272/2008 of the European Parliament and of the Council identifies the need for further information on the relevance of the metabolites IN-KE 121, IN-KF 313, M1; M2, M3, Polar B and Polars, the Member States concerned shall request the submission of such information. They shall ensure that the notifier provides that information to the Commission within six months from the notification of such a classification decision.</td></tr>
<tr><td>177</td><td>Oxadiazon<br>CAS No 19666-30-9<br>CIPAC No 213</td><td>5-tert-butyl-3-(2,4-dichloro-5-isopropoxyphenyl)-1,3,4-oxadiazol-2(3H)-one</td><td>≥ 940 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2018</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on oxadiazon, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 May 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured, which must be confirmed and supported by appropriate analytical data. The test material used in the toxicity dossiers shall be compared and verified against this specification of the technical material;<br>the potential for ground water contamination by the metabolite AE0608022 where the active substance is applied in situations for which prolonged anaerobic conditions may be expected to occur or in regions with vulnerable soil or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation must include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further studies to address the potential toxicological relevance of an impurity in the proposed technical specification;<br>information to further clarify the occurrence of metabolite AE0608033 in primary crops and rotational crops;<br>further trials on rotational crops (namely root crops and cereals) and a metabolism study on ruminants to confirm the consumer risk assessment;<br>information to further address the risk to earthworm-eating birds and mammals, and the long-term risk to fish.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 30 June 2012.</td></tr>
<tr><td>178</td><td>Picloram<br>CAS No 1918-02-1<br>CIPAC No 174</td><td>4-amino-3,5,6-trichloropyridine-2-carboxylic acid</td><td>≥ 920 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on picloram, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 May 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In the overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the potential for ground water contamination where picloram is applied in regions with vulnerable soil or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation must include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate;<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further information to confirm that the monitoring analytical method applied in residue trials correctly quantifies the residues of picloram and its conjugates;<br>a soil photolysis study to confirm the evaluation of picloram degradation.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 30 June 2012.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td></tr>
<tr><td>180</td><td>Bifenox<br>CAS No 42576-02-3<br>CIPAC No 413</td><td>Methyl 5-(2,4-dichlorophenoxy)-2-nitrobenzoate</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg impurities:<br>max. 3 g/kg 2,4-dichlorophenol<br>max. 6 g/kg 2,4-dichloroanisole</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on bifenox, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 14 March 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>((a)) the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment where appropriate;<br>((b)) the dietary exposure of consumers to bifenox residues in products of animal origin and in succeeding rotational crops;<br>((c)) the environmental conditions leading to the potential formation of nitrofen.<br>Member States shall impose restrictions as regards the conditions of use, where appropriate in view of point (c).</td></tr>
<tr><td>181</td><td>Diflufenican<br>CAS No 83164-33-4<br>CIPAC No 462</td><td>2′,4′-difluoro-2-(α,α,α-trifluoro-m-tolyloxy) nicotinanilide</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on diflufenican, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 14 March 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. Risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones shall be applied, where appropriate,<br>the protection of non-target plants. Risk mitigation measures such as an in-field no spray buffer zones shall be applied, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>182</td><td>Fenoxaprop-P<br>CAS No 113158-40-0<br>CIPAC No 484</td><td>(R)-2[4-[(6-chloro-2-benzoxazolyl)oxy]-phenoxy]-propanoic acid</td><td>≥ 920 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on fenoxaprop-P, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 14 March 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of non target plants,<br>the presence of the safener mefenpyr-diethyl in formulated products as regards operator, worker and bystander exposure,<br>the persistence of the substance and of some of its degradation products in colder zones and areas where anaerobic conditions may occur.<br>Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>183</td><td>Fenpropidin<br>CAS No 67306-00-7<br>CIPAC No 520</td><td>(R,S)-1-[3-(4-tert-butylphenyl)-2-methylpropyl]-piperidine</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg (racemate)</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on fenpropidin, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 14 March 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and must ensure that the conditions of authorisation include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures such as buffer zone.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of:<br>information to further address the long-term risk to herbivorous and insectivorous birds arising from the use of fenpropidin.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such confirmatory data and information to the Commission within two years from the approval.</td></tr>
<tr><td>184</td><td>Quinoclamine<br>CAS No 2797-51-5<br>CIPAC No 648</td><td>2-amino-3-chloro-1,4-naphthoquinone</td><td>≥ 965 g/kg impurity:<br>dichlone (2,3-dichloro-1,4-naphthoquinone) max. 15 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2018</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing quinoclamine for uses other than ornamentals or nursery plants, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on quinoclamine, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 14 March 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator, worker and bystander safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms,<br>the protection of birds and small mammals.<br>Conditions of use shall include adequate risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>185</td><td>Chloridazon<br>CAS No 1698-60-8<br>CIPAC No 111</td><td>5-amino-4-chloro-2-phenylpyridazin-3(2H)-one</td><td>920 g/kg<br>The manufacturing impurity 4-amino-5-chloro-isomer is considered to be of toxicological concern and a maximum level of 60 g/kg is established.</td><td>1 January 2009</td><td>31 December 2018</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide in application max. of 2,6 kg/ha only every third year on the same field may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on chloridazon, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 4 December 2007 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures and monitoring programmes should be initiated to verify potential groundwater contamination from metabolites B and B1 in vulnerable zones, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>186</td><td>Tritosulfuron<br>CAS No 142469-14-5<br>CIPAC No 735</td><td>1-(4-methoxy-6-trifluoromethyl-1,3,5-triazin-2-yl)-3-(2-trifluoromethyl-benzenesulfonyl)urea</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg<br>The following manufacturing impurity is of toxicological concern and must not exceed a certain amount in the technical material:<br>2-Amino-4-methoxy-6-(trifluormethyl)-1,3,5-triazine: &lt;0,2 g/kg</td><td>1 December 2008</td><td>30 November 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on tritosulfuron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 20 May 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the potential for groundwater contamination, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms,<br>the protection of small mammals.<br>Conditions of use shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>187</td><td>Flutolanil<br>CAS No 66332-96-5<br>CIPAC No 524</td><td>α,α,α-trifluoro-3′-isopropoxy-o-toluanilide</td><td>≥ 975 g/kg</td><td>1 March 2009</td><td>28 February 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing flutolanil for uses other than potato tuber treatment, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on flutolanil, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 20 May 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>188</td><td>Benfluralin<br>CAS No 1861-40-1<br>CIPAC No 285</td><td>N-butyl-N-ethyl-α,α,α-trifluoro-2,6-dinitro-p-toluidine</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg<br>Impurities:<br>ethyl-butyl-nitrosamine: max. 0,1 mg/kg</td><td>1 March 2009</td><td>28 February 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing benfluralin for uses other than lettuce and endive, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report onbenfluralin, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 20 May 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of the operators’ safety. Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment and risk mitigation measures to reduce the exposure,<br>the residues in food of plant and animal origin and evaluate the dietary exposure of consumers,<br>the protection of birds, mammals, surface waters and aquatic organisms. In relauion to these identified risks, sisk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, should be applied where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further studies on rotational crops metabolism and to confirm the risk assessment for metabolite B12 and for aquatic organisms. They shall ensure that the notifiers at whose request benfluralin has been included in this Annex provide such studies to the Commission within two years from the approval.</td></tr>
<tr><td>189</td><td>Fluazinam<br>CAS No 79622-59-6<br>CIPAC No 521</td><td>3-chloro-N-(3-chloro-5-trifluoromethyl-2-pyridyl)-α,α,α-trifluoro-2, 6-dinitro-p-toluidine</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg<br>Impurities:<br>5-chloro-N-(3-chloro-5-trifluoromethyl-2-pyridyl)-α,α,α-trifluoro-4,6-dinitro-o-toluidine<br>not more than 2 g/kg</td><td>1 March 2009</td><td>28 February 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing fluazinam for uses other than potatoes, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on fluazinam, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 20 May 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of the operators’ and workers’ safety. Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment and risk mitigation measures to reduce the exposure,<br>the residues in food of plant and animal origin and evaluate the dietary exposure of consumers,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. In relation to this identified risk, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, should be applied where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further studies to confirm the risk assessment for aquatic organisms and soil macro-organisms. They shall ensure that the notifiers at whose request fluazinam has been included in this Annex provide such studies to the Commission within two years from the approval.</td></tr>
<tr><td>190</td><td>Fuberidazole<br>CAS No 3878-19-1<br>CIPAC No 525</td><td>2-(2′-furyl)benzimidazole</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg</td><td>1 March 2009</td><td>28 February 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing fuberidazole for uses other than seed dressing, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on fuberidazole, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 20 May 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>long-term risk to mammals and must ensure that the conditions of authorisation include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.In such case the use of adequate equipment ensuring a high degree of incorporation in soil and a minimisation of spillage during application should apply.<br>Conditions of use shall include adequate risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>191</td><td>Mepiquat<br>CAS No 15302-91-7<br>CIPAC No 440</td><td>1,1-dimethylpiperidinium chloride (mepiquat chloride)</td><td>≥ 990 g/kg</td><td>1 March 2009</td><td>28 February 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as plant growth regulator may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing mepiquat for uses other than in barley, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on mepiquat, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 20 May 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>The Member States must pay particular attention to the residues in food of plant and animal origin and evaluate the dietary exposure of consumers.</td></tr>
<tr><td>192</td><td>Diuron<br>CAS No 330-54-1<br>CIPAC No 100</td><td>3-(3,4-dichlorophenyl)-1,1-dimethylurea</td><td>≥ 930 g/kg</td><td>1 October 2008</td><td>30 September 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide at rates not exceeding 0,5 kg/ha (areic average) may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on diuron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 July 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety; conditions of use shall prescribe the use of personal protective equipment, if appropriate,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and non-target plants.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>

<tr><td>210</td><td>Abamectin<br>CAS No 71751-41-2<br>avermectin B1a<br>CAS No 65195-55-3<br>Avermectin B1b<br>CAS No 65195-56-4<br>abamectin<br>CIPAC No 495</td><td>AvermectinB1a<br>(10E,14E,16E,22Z)-(1R,4S,5′S,6S,6′R,8R,12S,13S,20R,21R,24S)-6′-[(S)-sec-butyl]-21,24-dihydroxy-5′,11.13,22-tetramethyl-2-oxo-3.7,19-trioxatetracyclo[15.6.1.14,8 020,24]pentacosa-10.14,16,22-tetraene-6-spiro-2′-(5′,6′-dihydro-2′H-pyran)-12-yl 2,6-dideoxy-4-O-(2,6-dideoxy-3-O-methyl-α-L-arabino-hexopyranosyl)-3-O-methyl-α-L-arabino-hexopyranoside<br>AvermectinB1b<br>(10E,14E,16E,22Z)-(1R,4S,5′S,6S,6′R,8R,12S,13S,20R,21R,24S)-21,24-dihydroxy-6′-isopropyl-5′,11.13,22-tetramethyl-2-oxo-3.7,19-trioxatetracyclo[15.6.1.14,8 020,24]pentacosa-10.14,16,22-tetraene-6-spiro-2′-(5′,6′-dihydro-2′H-pyran)-12-yl 2,6-dideoxy-4-O-(2,6-dideoxy-3-O-methyl-α-L-arabino-hexopyranosyl)-3-O-methyl-α-L-arabino-hexopyranoside</td><td>≥ 850 g/kg</td><td>1 May 2009</td><td>30 April 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide, acaricide and nematicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing abamectin for uses other than citrus, lettuce and tomatoes, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information are provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on abamectin, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 July 2008 and of the addendum to the review report on abamectin, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on Plants, Animals, Food and Feed dated 24 January 2017 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the residues in food of plant origin and evaluate the dietary exposure of consumers,<br>the protection of bees, non-target arthropods, soil organisms, birds, mammals and aquatic organisms. In relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones and waiting periods, should be applied where appropriate.<br>The applicant shall submit to the Commission, the Member States and the Authority confirmatory information as regards the effect of water treatment processes on the nature of residues present in drinking water by two years after adoption of a guidance document on evaluation of the effect of water treatment processes on the nature of residues present in surface and groundwater.</td></tr>
<tr><td>211</td><td>Epoxiconazole<br>CAS No 135319-73-2 (formerly 106325-08-0)<br>CIPAC No 609</td><td>(2RS, 3SR)-1-[3-(2-chlorophenyl)-2,3-epoxy-2-(4-fluorophenyl)propyl]-1H-1.2,4-triazole</td><td>≥ 920 g/kg</td><td>1 May 2009</td><td>30 April 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on epoxiconazole, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 July 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment where appropriate,<br>the dietary exposure of consumers to the epoxiconazole (triazole) metabolites,<br>the potential for long-range transport via air,<br>the risk to aquatic organisms, birds and mammals. Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further studies addressing the potential endocrine disrupting properties of epoxiconazole within two years after the adoption of the OECD test guidelines on endocrine disruption or, alternatively, of Community agreed test guidelines.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier presents to the Commission not later than 30 June 2009 a monitoring programme to assess the long-range atmospheric transport of epoxiconazole and related environmental risks. The results of this monitoring shall be submitted as a monitoring report to the Commission by 31 December 2011 at the latest.<br>The concerned Member States shall ensure that the notifier submits within two years from the approval, at the latest, information on residues of epoxiconazole metabolites in primary crops, rotational crops and products of animal origin and information to further address the long-term risk to herbivorous birds and mammals.</td></tr>
<tr><td>212</td><td>Fenpropimorph<br>CAS No 67564-91-4<br>CIPAC No 427</td><td>(RS)-cis-4-[3-(4-tert-butylphenyl)-2-methylpropyl]-2,6-dimethylmorpholine</td><td>≥ 930 g/kg</td><td>1 May 2009</td><td>30 April 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on fenpropimorph, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 July 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operators and workers safety. Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment and risk mitigation measures to reduce the exposure, such as restrictions of the daily work rate,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate, such as buffer zones, reduction of run-off and drift reduction nozzles.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further studies to confirm the mobility in soil of the metabolite BF-421-7. They shall ensure that the notifiers at whose request fenpropimorph has been included in this Annex provide such studies to the Commission within two years from the approval.</td></tr>
<tr><td>213</td><td>Fenpyroximate<br>CAS No 134098-61-6<br>CIPAC No 695</td><td>tert-butyl (E)-alpha-(1,3-dimethyl-5-phenoxypyrazol-4-ylmethyleneamino-oxy)-p-toluate</td><td>&gt; 960 g/kg</td><td>1 May 2009</td><td>30 April 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as acaricide may be authorised.<br>The following uses must not be authorised:<br>applications in high crops with a high risk of spray drift, for example tractor mounted air-blast sprayer and hand-held applications.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on fenpyroximate, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 July 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the impact on aquatic organisms and non-target arthropods and must ensure that the conditions of authorisation include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of information to further address:<br>the risk to aquatic organisms from metabolites containing the benzyl moiety,<br>the risk of biomagnification in aquatic food chains.<br>They shall ensure that the notifiers at whose request fenpyroximate has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission within two years from the approval.</td></tr>
<tr><td>214</td><td>Tralkoxydim<br>CAS No 87820-88-0<br>CIPAC No 544</td><td>(RS)-2-[(EZ)-1-(ethoxyimino)propyl]-3-hydroxy-5-mesitylcyclohex-2-en-1-one</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg</td><td>1 May 2009</td><td>30 April 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on tralkoxydim, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 July 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of the groundwater, in particular from the soil metabolite R173642 when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions,<br>the protection of herbivorous mammals.<br>Conditions of use shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of:<br>information to further address the long-term risk to herbivorous mammals arising from the use of tralkoxydim.<br>They shall ensure that the notifiers at whose request tralkoxydim has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission within two years from the approval.</td></tr>
<tr><td>215</td><td>Aclonifen<br>CAS No 74070-46-5<br>CIPAC No 498</td><td>2-chloro-6-nitro-3-phenoxyaniline</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg<br>The impurity phenol is of toxicological concern and a maximum level of 5 g/kg is established.</td><td>1 August 2009</td><td>31 July 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing aclonifen for uses other than sunflower, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on aclonifen, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 September 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured, which must be confirmed and supported by appropriate analytical data. The test material used in the toxicity dossiers should be compared and verified against this specification of the technical material,<br>the protection of the operators safety. Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment and risk mitigation measures to reduce the exposure,<br>the residues in rotational crops and evaluate the dietary exposure of consumers,<br>the protection of birds, mammals, aquatic organisms and non-target plants. In relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, should be applied where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further studies on rotational crops residues and relevant information to confirm the risk assessment for birds, mammals, aquatic organisms and non-target plants.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such confirmatory data and information to the Commission within two years from the approval.</td></tr>
<tr><td>216</td><td>Imidacloprid<br>CAS No 138261-41-3<br>CIPAC No 582</td><td>(E)-1-(6-Chloro-3-pyridinylmethyl)-N-nitroimidazolidin-2-ylideneamine</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg</td><td>1 August 2009</td><td>1 December 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide, in permanent greenhouses or for the treatment of seeds intended to be used only in permanent greenhouses, may be authorised. The resulting crop must stay within a permanent greenhouse during its entire life cycle.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on imidacloprid, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 September 2008 and the conclusions of the revised addendum of the review report on imidacloprid as finalised in the Standing Committee on Plants, Animals, Food and Feed on 27 April 2018 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the risk to bees and bumble bees released for pollination in permanent greenhouses,<br>the impact on aquatic organisms,<br>the exposure of bees via the consumption of contaminated water from the permanent greenhouses.<br>Member States shall ensure that the seed coating shall only be performed in professional seed treatment facilities. Those facilities must apply the best available techniques in order to ensure that the release of dust during application to the seed, storage, and transport can be minimised.<br>Conditions of use shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>217</td><td>Metazachlor<br>CAS No 67129-08-2<br>CIPAC No 411</td><td>2-chloro-N-(pyrazol-1-ylmethyl)acet-2′,6′-xylidide</td><td>≥ 940 g/kg<br>The manufacturing impurity toluene is considered to be of toxicological concern and a maximum level of 0,05 % is established.</td><td>1 August 2009</td><td>31 July 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised. Applications shall be limited to a total dose of not more than 1,0 kg metazachlor/ha in a three-year period on the same field.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on metazachlor, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 September 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures and monitoring programmes shall be initiated to verify potential groundwater contamination from the metabolites 479M04, 479M08, 479M09, 479M11 and 479M12 in vulnerable zones, where appropriate.<br>If metazachlor is classified under Regularion (EC) No 1272/2008 as ‘suspected of causing cancer’, the Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the relevance of the metabolites 479M04, 479M08, 479M09, 479M11 and 479M12 with respect to cancer.<br>They shall ensure that the notifiers provide that information to the Commission within six months from the notification of such a classification decision.</td></tr>
<tr><td>218</td><td>Acetic acid<br>CAS No 64-19-7<br>CIPAC 838</td><td>acetic acid</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on acetic acid (SANCO/2602/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 16 July 2013 , shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to the protection of operators, the protection of groundwater and the protection of aquatic organisms.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The notifier shall submit confirmatory information as regards:<br>the acute and long-term risk to birds and mammals,<br>the risk to honeybees,<br>the risk to non-target arthropods.<br>The notifier shall submit that information to the Commission, the Member States and the Authority by 31 December 2015 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>219</td><td>Aluminium ammonium sulphate<br>CAS No 7784-26-1 (dodecahydrate), 7784-25-0 (anhydrous)<br>CIPAC No 840</td><td>Aluminium ammonium sulphate</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg (expressed as dodecahydrate)<br>≥ 502 g/kg (anhydrous)</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as repellent may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on aluminium ammonium sulphate (SANCO/2985/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 1 June 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The notifier shall submit confirmatory information as regards:<br>((a)) the impact on the environment of the transformation/dissociation products of aluminium ammonium sulphate;<br>((b)) the risk to non-target terrestrial organisms other than vertebrates and aquatic organisms.<br>This information shall be submitted to the Member States, the Commission and the Authority by 1 January 2016 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>220</td><td>Aluminium silicate<br>CAS No 1332-58-7<br>CIPAC No 841</td><td>Not available<br>Chemical name: Aluminium silicate</td><td>≥ 999,8 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as repellent may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on aluminium silicate (SANCO/2603/08) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 1 June 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to the operator safety; conditions of use shall include the application of adequate personal and respiratory protective equipment, where appropriate.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the applicant submits to the Commission confirmatory information as regards:<br>((a)) the specification of the technical material, as commercially manufactured, supported by appropriate analytical data;<br>((b)) the relevance of the test material used in the toxicity dossier in view of the specification of the technical material.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the applicant submits such information to the Commission by 1 May 2013 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>221</td><td>Ammonium acetate<br>CAS No 631-61-8<br>CIPAC No not allocated</td><td>Ammonium acetate</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg<br>Relevant impurity: Heavy metals as Pb maximum 10 ppm</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as attractant may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on ammonium acetate (SANCO/2986/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>

Blood meal must be in compliance with Regulation (EC) No 10Only uses as repellent may be authorised. Blood meal must be in compliance with Regulation (EC) No 1069/2009 and Regulation (EU) No 142/2011 .<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing blood meal for uses other than with direct application localised on individual plants, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorization is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles, as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the amended review report on blood meal (SANCO/2604/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 9 March 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The notifier shall submit to the Member States, the Commission and the Authority, confirmatory information on the specification of the technical material, by 1 March 2013 .</td></tr>

<tr><td>225</td><td>Carbon dioxide<br>CAS No: 124-38-9<br>CIPAC No: 844</td><td>Carbon dioxide</td><td>≥ 99,9 %<br>Relevant impurities:<br>phosphane max. 0,3 ppm v/v<br>benzene max. 0,02 ppm v/v<br>carbon monoxide max. 10 ppm v/v<br>methanol max. 10 ppm v/v<br>hydrogen cyanide max. 0,5 ppm v/v</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as a fumigant may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on carbon dioxide (SANCO/2987/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 17 May 2013 shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>

Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>
<tr><td>227</td><td>Ethylene<br>CAS No 74-85-1<br>CIPAC No 839</td><td>Ethylene</td><td>≥ 90 %<br>Relevant impurity: ethylene oxide, max content 1 mg/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only indoor uses as plant growth regulator by professional users may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on ethylene (SANCO/2608/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 1 February 2013 , shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>((a)) the compliance of ethylene with the required specifications, irrespective of the form in which it is supplied to the user;<br>((b)) the protection of operators, workers and bystanders.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>
<tr><td>228</td><td>Extract from tea tree<br>CAS No Tea Tree Oil 68647-73-4<br>Main components:<br>terpinen-4-ol 562-74-3<br>γ-terpinene 99-85-4<br>α-terpinene 99-86-5<br>1,8-cineole 470-82-6<br>CIPAC No 914</td><td>Tea Tree Oil is a complex mixture of chemical substances.</td><td>Main components:<br>terpinen-4-ol ≥ 300 g/kg<br>γ-terpinene ≥ 100 g/kg<br>α-terpinene ≥ 50 g/kg<br>1,8-cineole ≥ 1 g/kg<br>Relevant impurity:<br>Methyl eugenol: maximum 1 g/kg of the technical material</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide in greenhouse may be (SANCO/2609/2008 final) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 13 December 2013 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to<br>the protection of operators and workers, ensuring that conditions of use include the application of adequate personal protective equipment, where appropriate;<br>the protection of groundwater, when the substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions;<br>the protection of surface water and aquatic organisms;<br>the protection of honey bees, non-target arthropods, earthworms and non-target micro- and macro-organisms.<br>Conditions of use shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The notifier shall submit confirmatory information as regards:<br>((a)) the plant metabolism and consumer exposure;<br>((b)) the toxicity of the compounds that constitute the extract and the relevance of possible impurities other than methyl eugenol;<br>((c)) the groundwater exposure for the less strongly absorbed components that constitute the extract and for potential soil transformation products;<br>((d)) the effects on biological methods of sewage treatment.<br>The notifier shall submit to the Commission, the Member States and the Authority that information by 30 April 2016 at latest.</td></tr>

<tr><td>234</td><td>Hydrolysed proteins<br>CAS No not allocated<br>CIPAC No 901</td><td>Not available</td><td>Review report (SANCO/2615/2008)</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as attractant may be authorised. Hydrolysed proteins of animal origin must be in compliance with Regulation (EC) No 1069/2009 and Commission Regulation (EU) No 142/2011 .<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on hydrolysed proteins (SANCO/2615/08) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 1 June 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to the operator and worker safety; conditions of use shall include the application of adequate personal protective equipment, where appropriate.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the applicant submits to the Commission confirmatory information as regards:<br>((a)) the specifications of the technical material, as commercially manufactured, supported by appropriate analytical data;<br>((b)) the risk to aquatic organisms.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the applicant submits to the Commission the information set out in point (a) by 1 May 2013 , the information set out in point (b) by 1 November 2013 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>235</td><td><em>Iron sulphate</em> :<br>Iron(II)sulfate anhydrous: CAS No 7720-78-7<br>Iron(II)sulfate monohydrate: CAS No 17375-41-6<br>Iron(II)sulfate heptahydrate: CAS No 7782-63-0<br>CIPAC No 837</td><td>Iron(II)sulphate<br>or<br>iron(2+) sulfate</td><td>Iron(II)sulfate anhydrous: ≥ 350 g/kg total iron.<br>Relevant impurities:<br>arsenic, 18 mg/kg<br>cadmium, 1,8 mg/kg<br>chromium, 90 mg/kg<br>lead, 36 mg/kg<br>mercury, 1,8 mg/kg<br>expressed on the basis of the anhydrous variant</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles, as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the amended review report on iron sulphate (SANCO/2616/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 1 June 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>the risk for operator;<br>the risk to children/residents playing on treated turf;<br>the risk to surface waters and to aquatic organisms.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures and the application of adequate personal protective equipment. The notifier shall submit to the Member States, the Commission and the Authority, confirmatory information as regards the equivalence between the specifications of the technical material, as commercially manufactured, and those of the test material used in the toxicity dossiers.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission such information by 1 May 2013 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>236</td><td>Kieselgur (diatomaceous earth)<br>CAS No 61790-53-2<br>CIPAC No 647</td><td>Kieselgur (no IUPAC name)<br>Diatomaceous earth<br>Amorphous silicon dioxide<br>Silica<br>Diatomite</td><td>The product consists of 100 % diatomaceous earth.<br>Maximum 0,1 % of particles of crystalline silica with diameter below 50 μm</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only indoor uses as insecticide and acaricide by professional users may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on kieselgur (diatomaceous earth) (SANCO/2617/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 3 October 2013 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to the safety of operators and workers. Conditions of use shall include the application of adequate personal and respiratory protective equipment. Where necessary, conditions of use shall prohibit the presence of workers after application of the product concerned for a period appropriate in view of the risks caused by that product.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifiers submit, by 25 November 2015 , to the Commission, the Member States and the Authority information concerning the inhalation toxicity to confirm the occupational limits of kieselgur (diatomaceous earth).</td></tr>
<tr><td>237</td><td>Limestone<br>CAS No: 1317-65-3<br>CIPAC No: 852</td><td>Calcium carbonate</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as repellent may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles, as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the amended review report on limestone (SANCO/2618/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 9 March 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td></tr>
<tr><td>239</td><td>Pepper dust extraction residue (PDER)<br>CAS No: not allocated<br>CIPAC No: not allocated</td><td>Steam distilled and solvent extracted Black pepper – <em>Piper nigrum</em></td><td>It is a complex mixture of chemical substances, the component piperine as marker should be minimum 4 %</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as repellent may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing pepper dust extraction residue (PDER) for uses other than in home garden area, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorization is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles, as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the amended review report on pepper (SANCO/2620/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 9 March 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures<br>The notifier shall submit to the Member States, the Commission and the Authority, confirmatory information on the specification of the technical material, by 1 March 2013 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>240</td><td>Plant oils/citronella oil<br>CAS No 8000-29-1<br>CIPAC No 905</td><td>Citronella Oil is a complex mixture of chemical substances.<br>The main components are:<br>Citronellal (3,7-dimethyl-6-octenal).<br>Geraniol ((E)-3,7-dimethyl-2,6-octadien-1-ol).<br>Citronellol (3,7-dimethyl-6-octan-2-ol).<br>Geranyl acetate (3,7-dimethyl-6-octen-1yl acetate).</td><td>The sum of the following impurities must not exceed 0,1 % of technical material: methyl eugenol and methyl-isoeugenol.</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on citronella oil (SANCO/2621/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of operators, workers, bystanders and residents, ensuring that conditions of use include the application of adequate personal protective equipment, where appropriate;<br>the protection of groundwater, when the substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil;<br>the risk to non-target organisms.<br>The notifier shall submit confirmatory information as regards:<br>((a)) the technical specification;<br>((b)) data comparing natural background exposure situations of plant oils/citronella oil and methyl eugenol and methyl isoeugenol in relation to exposure from the use of plant oils/citronella oil as a plant protection product. This data shall cover human exposure as well as exposure of non-target organisms;<br>((c)) the groundwater exposure assessment for potential metabolites of plant oils/citronella oil, in particular for methyl eugenol and methyl isoeugenol.<br>The notifier shall submit to the Commission, the Member States and the Authority that information by 30 April 2016 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>241</td><td>Plant oils/clove oil<br>CAS No 84961-50-2 (clove oil)<br>97-53-0 (Eugenol — main component)<br>CIPAC No 906</td><td>Clove Oil is a complex mixture of chemical substances.<br>The main component is eugenol.</td><td>≥ 800 g/kg<br>Relevant impurity: methyl eugenol maximum 0,1 % of the technical material</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only indoor uses as post-harvest fungicide and bactericide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on clove oil (SANCO/2622/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to the protection of operators and workers ensuring that conditions of use include the application of adequate personal protective equipment, where appropriate.<br>The notifier shall submit confirmatory information as regards<br>((a)) the technical specification;<br>((b)) data comparing natural background exposure situations of plant oils/clove oil, eugenol and methyl eugenol in relation to exposure from the use of plant oils/clove oil as a plant protection product. This data shall cover human exposure.<br>The notifier shall submit to the Commission, the Member States and the Authority that information by 30 April 2016 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>242</td><td>Plant oils/rape seed oil<br>CAS No: 8002-13-9<br>CIPAC No: not allocated</td><td>Rape seed oil</td><td>Rape seed oil is a complex mixture of fatty acids<br>Relevant impurity: Maximum 2 % of erucic acid</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide and acaricide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on rape seed oil (SANCO/2623/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 3 October 2013 shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>
<tr><td>243</td><td>Plant oils/spear mint oil<br>CAS No 8008-79-5<br>CIPAC No 908</td><td>Spearmint oil</td><td>≥ 550 g/kg as (R)-Carvone</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as plant growth regulator for postharvest treatment of potatoes may be authorised.<br>Member States shall ensure that authorisations provide that hot fogging is performed exclusively in professional storage facilities and that the best available techniques are applied to exclude the release into the environment of the product (fogging mist) during storage, transport, waste disposal and application.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles, as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the amended review report on plant oils/spearmint oil (SANCO/2624/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 1 June 2012 shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>

Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>

29-9<br>Cinerin 1: CAS 25402- 06-6<br>Cinerin 2: CAS 121- 20-0<br>Jasmolin 1: CAS 4466- 14-2<br>Jasmolin 2: CAS 1172- 63-0<br>Extract B:<br>Pyrethrin 1: CAS 121-21-1<br>Pyrethrin 2: CAS 121- 29-9<br>Cinerin 1: CAS 25402- 06-6<br>Cinerin 2: CAS 121- 20-0<br>Jasmolin 1: CAS 4466- 14-2<br>Jasmolin 2: CAS 1172- 63-0</td><td>Pyrethrins are a complex mixture of chemical substances.</td><td>Extract A: ≥ 500 g/kg Pyrethrins<br>Extract B: ≥ 480 g/kg Pyrethrins</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide may be (SANCO/2627/2008) and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>((a)) the risk to operators and workers;<br>((b)) the risk to non-target organisms.<br>Conditions of use shall, where appropriate, include the
