<tr><td>260</td><td>Aluminium phosphide<br>CAS No 20859-73-8<br>CIPAC No 227</td><td>Aluminium phosphide</td><td>≥ 830 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide, rodenticide, talpicide and leporicide in the form of ready-to-use aluminium phosphide containing products may be authorised.<br>As rodenticide, talpicide and leporicide only outdoor uses may be authorised.<br>Authorisations should be limited to professional users.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on aluminium phosphide, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of consumers and ensure that the spent ready-to-use aluminium phosphide containing products are removed from the food commodity in uses against storage pests and subsequently an adequate additional withholding period is applied;<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal and respiratory protective equipment;<br>the protection of operators and workers during fumigation for indoor uses;<br>the protection of workers at re-entry (after fumigation period) for indoor uses;<br>the protection of bystanders against leaking of gas for indoor uses;<br>the protection of birds and mammals. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, such as the closure of the burrows and the achievement of complete incorporation of granules in the soil, where appropriate;<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones between treated areas and surface water bodies, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>261</td><td>Calcium phosphide<br>CAS No 1305-99-3<br>CIPAC No 505</td><td>Calcium phosphide</td><td>≥ 160 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>1 December 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only outdoor uses as rodenticide and talpicide in the form of ready-to-use calcium phosphide containing products may be authorised.<br>Authorisations should be limited to professional users.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on calcium phosphide, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal and respiratory protective equipment;<br>the protection of birds and mammals. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, such as the closure of the burrows and the achievement of complete incorporation of granules in the soil, where appropriate;<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones between treated areas and surface water bodies, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>262</td><td>Magnesium phosphide<br>CAS No 12057-74-8<br>CIPAC No 228</td><td>Magnesium phosphide</td><td>≥ 880 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide, rodenticide, talpicide and leporicide in the form of ready-to-use magnesium phosphide containing products may be authorised.<br>As rodenticide, talpicide and leporicide only outdoor uses may be authorised.<br>Authorisations should be limited to professional users.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on magnesium phosphide, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of consumers and ensure that the spent ready-to-use magnesium phosphide containing products are removed from the food commodity in uses against storage pests and subsequently an adequate additional withholding period is applied;<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal and respiratory protective equipment;<br>the protection of operators and workers during fumigation for indoor uses;<br>the protection of workers at re-entry (after fumigation period) for indoor uses;<br>the protection of bystanders against leaking of gas for indoor uses;<br>the protection of birds and mammals. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, such as the closure of the burrows and the achievement of complete incorporation of granules in the soil, where appropriate;<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones between treated areas and surface water bodies, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>263</td><td>Cymoxanil<br>CAS No 57966-95-7<br>CIPAC No 419</td><td>1-[(E/Z)-2-cyano-2-methoxyiminoacetyl]-3-ethylurea</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on cymoxanil, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment;<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions;<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and must ensure that the conditions of authorisation include risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>264</td><td>Dodemorph<br>CAS No 1593-77-7<br>CIPAC No 300</td><td>cis/trans-[4-cyclododecyl]-2,6-dimethylmorpholine</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide on ornamentals in glasshouse may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on dodemorph, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment where appropriate;<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil conditions;<br>conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>265</td><td>2,5-Dichlorobenzoic acid methylester<br>CAS No 2905-69-3<br>CIPAC No 686</td><td>methyl-2,5-dichlorobenzoate</td><td>≥ 995 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only indoor uses as plant growth regulator and fungicide for grafting of grapevines may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on 2,5-Dichlorobenzoic acid methylester, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.</td></tr>
<tr><td>266</td><td>Metamitron<br>CAS No 41394-05-2<br>CIPAC No 381</td><td>4-amino-4,5-dihydro-3-methyl-6-phenyl-1,2,4-triazin-5-one</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing metamitron for uses other than on root crops, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on metamitron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of personal protective equipment where appropriate;<br>the protection of groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions;<br>the risk to birds and mammals, and non-target terrestrial plants.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the impact of soil metabolite M3 on groundwater, on residues in rotational crops, on the long-term risk to insectivorous birds and the specific risk to birds and mammals that may be contaminated by the intake of water in field. They shall ensure that the notifiers at whose request metamitron has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission by 31 August 2011 at the latest.</td></tr>
<tr><td>267</td><td>Sulcotrione<br>CAS No 99105-77-8<br>CIPAC No 723</td><td>2-(2-chloro-4-mesylbenzoyl)cyclohexane-1,3-dione</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg<br>Impurities:<br>hydrogen cyanide: not more than 80 mg/kg<br>toluene: not more than 4 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on sulcotrione, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment where appropriate;<br>the risk to insectivorous birds, aquatic and terrestrial non-target plants, and non-target arthropods.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the degradation in soil and water of the cyclohexadione moiety and the long-term risk to insectivorous birds. They shall ensure that the notifier at whose request sulcotrione has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission by 31 August 2011 at the latest.</td></tr>
<tr><td>268</td><td>Tebuconazole<br>CAS No 107534-96-3<br>CIPAC No 494</td><td>(RS)-1-p-chlorophenyl-4,4-dimethyl-3-(1H-1,2,4-triazol-1-ylmethyl)-pentan-3-ol</td><td>≥ 905 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide and plant growth regulator may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on tebuconazole, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account. In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment;<br>the dietary exposure of consumers to the tebuconazole (triazole) metabolites;<br>the potential for groundwater contamination, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil or climatic conditions, in particular as regards the occurrence in groundwater of the metabolite 1,2,4-triazole;<br>the protection of granivorous birds and mammals and herbivorous mammals and must ensure that the conditions of authorisation include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures;<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and must ensure that conditions of authorisation include risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information addressing the potential endocrine disrupting properties of tebuconazole within two years after the adoption of the OECD test guidelines on endocrine disruption or, alternatively, of Community agreed test guidelines.</td></tr>
<tr><td>269</td><td>Triadimenol<br>CAS No 55219-65-3<br>CIPAC No 398</td><td>(1RS,2RS;1RS,2SR)-1-(4-chlorophenoxy)-3,3-dimethyl-1-(1H-1,2,4-triazol-1-yl)butan-2-ol</td><td>≥ 920 g/kg<br>isomer A (1RS,2SR), isomer B (1RS,2RS)<br>Diastereomer A, RS + SR, range: 70 to 85 %<br>Diastereomer B, RR + SS, range: 15 to 30 %</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on triadimenol, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the presence of N-methylpyrrolidone in formulated products as regards operator, worker and bystander exposure;<br>the protection of birds and mammals. In relation to these identified risks risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, should be applied where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further information on the specification;<br>information to further address the risk assessment for birds and mammals.<br>information to further address the risk of endocrine disrupting effects on fish.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier at whose request triadimenol has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission by 31 August 2011 at the latest.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information addressing the potential endocrine disrupting properties of triadimenol within two years after the adoption of the OECD test guidelines on endocrine disruption or, alternatively, of Community agreed test guidelines.</td></tr>
<tr><td>270</td><td>Methomyl<br>CAS No 16752-77-50<br>CIPAC No 264</td><td>S-methyl (EZ)-N-(methylcarbamoyloxy)thioacetimidate</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg</td><td>1 September 2009</td><td>31 August 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide on vegetables may be authorised at rates not exceeding 0,25 kg active substance per hectare per application and for a maximum of 2 applications per season.<br>Authorisations shall be limited to professional users.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on methomyl, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 12 June 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety: conditions of use shall prescribe the use of adequate personal protective equipment. Special attention shall be paid to the exposure of operators using knapsacks or other hand-held application equipment,<br>the protection of birds,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms: conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate, such as buffer zones, reduction of run-off and drift reduction nozzles,<br>the protection of non-target arthropods, in particular bees: risk mitigation measures to avoid all contact with bees shall be applied.<br>Member States shall ensure that methomyl-based formulations contain effective repelling and/or emetic agents.<br>Where appropriate, conditions of authorisation shall include further risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>
<tr><td>271</td><td>Bensulfuron<br>CAS No 83055-99-6<br>CIPAC No 502.201</td><td>α-[(4,6-dimethoxypyrimidin-2-ylcarbamoyl)sulfamoyl]-o-toluic acid (bensulfuron)<br>methyl α-[(4,6-dimethoxypyrimidin-2-ylcarbamoyl)sulfamoyl]-o-toluate (bensulfuron-methyl)</td><td>≥ 975 g/kg</td><td>1 November 2009</td><td>31 October 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as a herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on bensulfuron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 8 December 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to the following:<br>the protection of aquatic organisms; in relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, shall be applied where appropriate,<br>the protection of the groundwater, where the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further studies on the specification,<br>information to further address the route and rate of degradation of bensulfuron-methyl under aerobic flooded soil conditions,<br>information to address the relevance of metabolites for the consumer risk assessment.<br>They shall ensure that the notifiers provide such studies to the Commission by 31 October 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>272</td><td>Sodium 5-nitroguaiacolate<br>CAS No 67233-85-6<br>CIPAC number not allocated</td><td>Sodium 2-methoxy-5-nitrophenolate</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg</td><td>1 November 2009</td><td>31 October 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only use as plant growth regulator may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on sodium 5-nitroguaiacolate, sodium o-nitrophenolate and sodium p-nitrophenolate, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 2 December 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured, which must be confirmed and supported by appropriate analytical data. The test material used in the toxicity dossiers should be compared and verified against this specification of the technical material,<br>the protection of the operators and workers safety. Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment and risk mitigation measures to reduce the exposure,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further studies to address the risk to groundwater. They shall ensure that the notifiers provide such studies to the Commission by 31 October 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>273</td><td>Sodium o-nitrophenolate<br>CAS No 824-39-5<br>CIPAC number not allocated</td><td>Sodium 2-nitrophenolate; sodium o-nitrophenolate</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg<br>The following impurities are of toxicological concern:<br>Phenol<br>Max content: 0,1 g/kg<br>2,4 dinitrophenol<br>max content: 0,14 g/kg<br>2,6 dinitrophenol<br>max content: 0,32 g/kg</td><td>1 November 2009</td><td>31 October 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only use as plant growth regulator may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on sodium 5-nitroguaiacolate, sodium o-nitrophenolate and sodium p-nitrophenolate, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 2 December 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured, which must be confirmed and supported by appropriate analytical data. The test material used in the toxicity dossiers should be compared and verified against this specification of the technical material,<br>the protection of the operators and workers safety. Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment and risk mitigation measures to reduce the exposure,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further studies to address the risk to groundwater. They shall ensure that the notifiers provide such studies to the Commission by 31 October 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>274</td><td>Sodium p-nitrophenolate<br>CAS No 824-78-2<br>CIPAC number not allocated</td><td>Sodium 4-nitrophenolate; sodium p-nitrophenolate</td><td>≥ 998 g/kg<br>The following impurities are of toxicological concern:<br>Phenol<br>max content: 0,1 g/kg<br>2,4 dinitrophenol<br>max content: 0,07 g/kg<br>2,6 dinitrophenol<br>max content: 0,09 g/kg</td><td>1 November 2009</td><td>31 October 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only use as plant growth regulator may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on sodium 5-nitroguaiacolate, sodium o-nitrophenolate and sodium p-nitrophenolate, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 2 December 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured, which must be confirmed and supported by appropriate analytical data. The test material used in the toxicity dossiers should be compared and verified against this specification of the technical material,<br>the protection of the operators and workers safety. Authorised conditions of use must prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment and risk mitigation measures to reduce the exposure,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation should include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further studies to address the risk to groundwater. They shall ensure that the notifiers provide such studies to the Commission by 31 October 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>275</td><td>Tebufenpyrad<br>CAS No 119168-77-3<br>CIPAC No 725</td><td>N-(4-tert-butylbenzyl)-4-chloro-3-ethyl-1-methylpyrazole-5-carboxamide</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg</td><td>1 November 2009</td><td>31 October 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as acaricide and insecticide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing tebufenpyrad in formulations other than water soluble bags Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on tebufenpyrad, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 2 December 2008 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and must ensure that conditions of authorisation include risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones, where appropriate,<br>the protection of insectivorous birds and must ensure that the conditions of authorisation include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further information confirming that no relevant impurities are present,<br>information to further address the risk to insectivorous birds.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 31 October 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>276</td><td>Chlormequat<br>CAS No 7003-89-6 (chlormequat)<br>CAS No 999-81-5 (chlormequat chloride)<br>CIPAC No 143 (chlormequat)<br>CIPAC No 143.302 (chlormequat chloride)</td><td>2-chloroethyltrimethylammonium (chlormequat)<br>2-chloroethyltrimethylammonium chloride<br>(chlormequat chloride)</td><td>≥ 636 g/kg<br>Impurities<br>1,2-dichloroethane: max 0,1 g/kg (on the dry chlormequat chloride content)<br>Chloroethene (vinylchloride): max 0,0005 g/kg (on the dry chlormequat chloride content)</td><td>1 December 2009</td><td>30 November 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as plant growth regulator on cereals and non edible crops may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing chlormequat for uses other than in rye and triticale, notably as regards the exposure of consumers, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on chlormequat, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 23 January 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment;<br>the protection of birds and mammals.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the fate and behaviour (adsorption studies to be performed at 20 °C, recalculation of the predicted concentrations in groundwater, surface water and sediment), the monitoring methods for determination of the substance in animal products and water, and the risk to aquatic organisms, birds and mammals. They shall ensure that the notifier at whose request chlormequat has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission by 30 November 2011 at the latest.</td></tr>

The test material used in the toxicity dossiers should be compared and verified against this specification of the technical material,<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and non-target plants and ensure that conditions of authorisation include risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones, where appropriate,<br>the protection of non-target arthropods and ensure that the conditions of authorisation include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further information on the relevant impurity Ro 41-5259,<br>information to further address the risk to aquatic organisms and to non-target arthropods.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 30 November 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="3">279</td><td colspan="4">Quizalofop-P</td><td></td><td></td></tr>
<tr><td>Quizalofop-P-tefuryl<br>CAS No 119738-06-6<br>CIPAC No 641.226</td><td>(RS)-Tetrahydrofurfuryl (R)-2-[4-(6-chloroquinoxalin-2-yloxy)phenoxy]propionate</td><td>≥ 795 g/kg</td><td>1 December 2009</td><td>30 November 2021</td><td rowspan="2">PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on quizalofop-P, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 23 January 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured which must be confirmed and supported by appropriate analytical data. The test material used in the toxicity dossiers should be compared and verified against this specification of the technical material,<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of non-target plants and ensure that conditions of authorisation include risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones, where appropriate.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information on the risk to non-target arthropods.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 30 November 2011 .</td></tr>
<tr><td>Quizalofop-P-ethyl<br>CAS No 100646-51-3<br>CIPAC No 641.202</td><td>ethyl (R)-2-[4-(6-chloroquinoxalin-2-yloxy)phenoxy]propionate</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg</td><td>1 December 2009</td><td>30 November 2021</td></tr>
<tr><td>280</td><td>Teflubenzuron<br>CAS No 83121-18-0<br>CIPAC No 450</td><td>1-(3,5-dichloro-2,4-difluorophenyl)-3-(2,6-difluorobenzoyl)urea</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg</td><td>1 December 2009</td><td>30 November 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide in glasshouses (on artificial substrate or closed hydroponic systems) may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing teflubenzuron for uses other than on tomatoes in greenhouses, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on teflubenzuron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 23 January 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and workers safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment, where appropriate,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. Releases from glasshouse application must be minimised and, in any case, should not have the potential to reach in significant levels water bodies in the vicinity,<br>the protection of bees which should be prevented from accessing the glasshouse,<br>the protection of pollinator colonies purposely placed in the glasshouse,<br>the safe disposal of condensation water, drain water and substrate in order to preclude risks to non-target organisms and contamination of surface water and groundwater.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>281</td><td>Zeta-cypermethrin<br>CAS No 52315-07-8<br>CIPAC No 733</td><td>Mixture of the stereoisomers (S)-α-cyano-3-phenoxybenzyl (1RS,3RS;1RS,3SR)-3-(2,2-dichlorovinyl)-2,2 dimethylcyclopropanecarboxylate where the ratio of the (S);(1RS,3RS) isomeric pair to the (S);(1RS,3SR) isomeric pair lies in the ratio range 45-55 to 55-45 respectively</td><td>≥ 850 g/kg<br>Impurities:<br>toluene: max 2 g/kg<br>tars: max 12,5 g/kg</td><td>1 December 2009</td><td>1 December 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing zeta-cypermethrin for uses other than in cereals, notably as regards the exposure of consumers to mPBAldehyde, a degradation product that may be formed during processing, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on zeta-cypermethrin, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 23 January 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment, where appropriate,<br>the protection of birds, aquatic organisms, bees, non-target arthropods and non-target soil macro-organisms.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the fate and behaviour (aerobic degradation in soil), the long-term risk to birds, aquatic organisms and non-target arthropods. They shall ensure that the notifier at whose request zeta-cypermethrin has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission by 30 November 2011 at the latest.</td></tr>
<tr><td>282</td><td>Chlorsulfuron<br>CAS No 64902-72-3<br>CIPAC No 391</td><td>1-(2-chlorophenylsulfonyl)-3-(4-methoxy-6-methyl-1,3,5-triazin-2-yl)urea</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg<br>Impurities:<br>2-Chlorobenzenesulfonamide (IN-A4097) not more than 5 g/kg and<br>4-methoxy-6-methyl-1,3,5-triazin-2-amine (IN-A4098) not more than 6 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on chlorsulfuron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and non-target plants; in relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, shall be applied where appropriate,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>The Member States concerned shall:<br>ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further studies on the specification by 1 January 2010.<br>If chlorsulfuron is classified as carcinogenic category 2 in accordance with Regulation (EC) No 1272/2008, the Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the relevance of the metabolites IN-A4097, IN-A4098, IN-JJ998, IN-B5528 and IN-V7160 with respect to cancer and ensure that the notifier provides that information to the Commission within six months from the notification of the classification decision concerning that substance.</td></tr>
<tr><td>283</td><td>Cyromazine<br>CAS No 66215-27-8<br>CIPAC No 420</td><td>N-cyclopropyl-1,3,5-triazine-2,4,6-triamine</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide in greenhouses may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>In assessing applications to authorise plant protection products containing cyromazine for uses other than in tomatoes, notably as regards the exposure of consumers, Member States shall pay particular attention to the criteria in Article 4(3) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, and shall ensure that any necessary data and information is provided before such an authorisation is granted.<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on cyromazine, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms,<br>the protection of pollinators.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the fate and behaviour of the soil metabolite NOA 435343 and on the risk to aquatic organisms. They shall ensure that the notifier at whose request cyromazine has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission by 31 December 2011 at the latest.</td></tr>
<tr><td>284</td><td>Dimethachlor<br>CAS No 50563-36-5<br>CIPAC No 688</td><td>2-chloro-N-(2-methoxyethyl)acet-2′,6′-xylidide</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg<br>Impurity 2,6-dimethylaniline: Not more than 0,5 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide in application max. of 1,0 kg/ha only every third year on the same field may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on dimethachlor, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and non-target plants; in relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, shall be applied where appropriate,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures and monitoring programmes shall be initiated to verify potential groundwater contamination from metabolites CGA 50266, CGA 354742, CGA 102935 and SYN 528702 in vulnerable zones, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall:<br>ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further studies on the specification by 1 January 2010.<br>If dimethachlor is classified as carcinogenic category 2 in accordance with Regulation (EC) No 1272/2008, the Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the relevance of the metabolites CGA 50266, CGA 354742, CGA 102935 and SYN 528702 with respect to cancer and ensure that the notifier provides that information to the Commission within six months from the notification of the classification decision concerning that substance.</td></tr>
<tr><td>285</td><td>Etofenprox<br>CAS No 80844-07-1<br>CIPAC No 471</td><td>2-(4-ethoxyphenyl)-2-methylpropyl 3-phenoxybenzyl<br>ether</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on etofenprox, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms; in relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, shall be applied where appropriate,<br>the protection of bees and non-target arthropods; in relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, shall be applied where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall:<br>ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information on the risk to aquatic organisms including the risk to sediment dwellers and biomagnification,<br>the submission of further studies on the endocrine disruption potential in aquatic organisms (fish full life cycle study).<br>They shall ensure that the notifiers provide such studies to the Commission by 31 December 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>286</td><td>Lufenuron<br>CAS No 103055-07-8<br>CIPAC No 704</td><td>(RS)-1-[2,5-dichloro-4-(1,1,2,3,3,3-hexafluoro-propoxy)-phenyl]-3-(2,6-difluorobenzoyl)-urea</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only indoor uses or use in outdoor bait stations as insecticide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on lufenuron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the high persistency in the environment and the high risk for bioaccumulation and shall ensure that the use of lufenuron has no adverse long-term effects on non-target organisms,<br>the protection of birds, mammals, soil non-target organisms, bees, non-target arthropods, surface waters and aquatic organisms in vulnerable situations.<br>The Member States concerned shall:<br>ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further studies on the specification by 1 January 2010.</td></tr>
<tr><td>287</td><td>Penconazole<br>CAS No 66246-88-6<br>CIPAC No 446</td><td>(RS) 1-[2-(2,4-dichloro-phenyl)-pentyl]-1H-[1,2,4] triazole</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicides may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on penconazole, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the fate and behaviour of the soil metabolite CGA179944 in acidic soils. They shall ensure that the notifier at whose request penconazole has been included in this Annex provide such information to the Commission by 31 December 2011 at the latest.</td></tr>
<tr><td>288</td><td>Tri-allate<br>CAS No 2303-17-5<br>CIPAC No 97</td><td>S-2,3,3-trichloroallyl di-isopropyl<br>(thiocarbamate)</td><td>≥ 940 g/kg<br>NDIPA (Nitroso-diisopropylamine)<br>max. 0,02 mg/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on tri-allate, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the operator safety and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the dietary exposure of consumers to residues of tri-allate in treated crops as well as in succeeding rotational crops and in products of animal origin<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and non-target plants and ensure that conditions of authorisation include risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones, where appropriate,<br>the potential for ground water contamination by the degradation products TCPSA when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation must include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further information to assess the primary plant metabolism,<br>further information on the fate and behaviour of the soil metabolite diisopropylamine,<br>further information on the potential for biomagnification in aquatic food chains,<br>information to further address the risk to fish-eating mammals and the long-term risk to earthworms.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 31 December 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>289</td><td>Triflusulfuron<br>CAS No 126535-15-7<br>CIPAC No 731</td><td>2-[4-dimethylamino-6-(2,2,2-trifluoroethoxy)-1,3,5-triazin-2-ylcarbamoylsulfamoyl]-m-toluic acid</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as a herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on triflusulfuron, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the dietary exposure of consumers to residues of metabolites IN-M7222 and IN-E7710 in succeeding rotational crops and in products of animal origin,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and aquatic plants from the risk arising from triflusulfuron and the metabolite IN-66036 and ensure that conditions of authorisation include risk mitigation measures such as buffer zones, where appropriate,<br>the potential for ground water contamination by the degradation products IN-M7222 and IN-W6725 when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions. Conditions of authorisation must include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>If triflusulfuron is classified as carcinogenic category 2 in accordance with Regulation (EC) No 1272/2008, the Member States concerned shall request the submission of further information on the relevance of the metabolites IN-M7222, IN-D8526 and IN-E7710 with respect to cancer. They shall ensure that the notifier provides that information to the Commission within six months from the notification of the classification decision concerning that substance.</td></tr>
<tr><td>290</td><td>Difenacoum<br>CAS No 56073-07-5<br>CIPAC No 514</td><td>3-[(1RS,3RS;1RS,3SR)-3-biphenyl-4-yl-1,2,3,4-tetrahydro-1-naphthyl]-4-hydroxycoumarin</td><td>≥ 905 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>30 December 2019</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as rodenticide in the form of pre-prepared baits placed in specially constructed, tamper resistant and secured bait boxes are authorised.<br>The nominal concentration of the active substance in the products shall not exceed 50 mg/kg.<br>Authorisations shall be limited to professional users.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on difenacoum, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account. In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to the protection of birds and non-target mammals from primary and secondary poisoning. Risk mitigation measures shall be applied where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information on methods for the determination of residues of difenacoum in body fluids.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 30 November 2011.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information on the specification of the active substance as manufactured.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 31 December 2009.</td></tr>
<tr><td></td></tr>
<tr><td>292</td><td>sulphur<br>CAS No 7704-34-9<br>CIPAC No 18</td><td>sulphur</td><td>≥ 990 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide and acaricide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on sulphur, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 12 March 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of birds, mammals, aquatic organisms and non-target arthropods. Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submit to the Commission further information to confirm the risk assessment for birds, mammals, sediment dwelling organisms and non-target arthropods. They shall ensure that the notifier at whose request sulphur has been included in this Annex provide such data to the Commission at latest by 30 June 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>293</td><td>Tetraconazole<br>CAS No 112281-77-3<br>CIPAC No 726</td><td>(RS)-2-(2,4-dichlorophenyl)-3-(1H-1.2,4-triazol-1-yl)-propyl-1.1,2,2-tetrafluoroethyl ether</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg (racemic mixture)<br>Impurity toluene: not more than 13 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on tetraconazole, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 26 February 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of aquatic organisms and non-target plants; in relation to these identified risks, risk mitigation measures, such as buffer zones, shall be applied where appropriate,<br>the protection of the groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>The Member States concerned shall request:<br>the submission of further information on a refined consumer risk assessment,<br>further information on the specification regarding ecotoxicology,<br>further information on the fate and behaviour of potential metabolites in all relevant compartments,<br>the refined risk assessment of such metabolites to birds, mammals aquatic organisms and non-target arthropods,<br>further information on the potential for endocrine disrupting effects to birds, mammals and fish.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 31 December 2011.</td></tr>
<tr><td>294</td><td>Paraffin oils<br>CAS No 64742-46-7<br>CAS No 72623-86-0<br>CAS No 97862-82-3<br>CIPAC No n.a.</td><td>paraffin oil</td><td>European Pharmacopoeia 6.0</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide and acaricide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on paraffin oils CAS No 64742-46-7, CAS No 72623-86-0 and CAS No 97862-82-3, and in particular Appendices I and II thereto shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The Member States concerned shall request:<br>the submission of the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured to verify the compliance with purity criteria of European Pharmacopoeia. 6.0.<br>They shall ensure that the notifiers provides such information to the Commission by 30 June 2010.</td></tr>
<tr><td>295</td><td>Paraffin oil<br>CAS No 8042-47-5<br>CIPAC No n.a.</td><td>paraffin oil</td><td>European Pharmacopoeia. 6,0</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide and acaricide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on paraffin oil 8042-47-5, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, shall be taken into account.<br>Conditions of use shall include, where appropriate, risk mitigation measures.<br>The Member States concerned shall request:<br>The submission of the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured to verify the compliance with purity criteria of European Pharmacopoeia. 6,0<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 30 June 2010.</td></tr>
<tr><td>296</td><td>Cyflufenamid<br>CAS No 180409-60-3<br>CIPAC No 759</td><td>(Z)-N-[α-(cyclopropylmethoxyimino) – 2,3-difluoro-6-(trifluoromethyl)benzyl]-2-phenylacetamide</td><td>&gt; 980 g/kg</td><td>1 April 2010</td><td>31 March 2023</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on cyflufenamid, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 2 October 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention to the protection of groundwater, when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>297</td><td>Fluopicolide<br>CAS No<br>239110-15-7<br>CIPAC No 787</td><td>2,6-dichloro-N-[3-chloro-5-(trifluoromethyl)-2-pyridylmethyl]benzamide</td><td>≥ 970 g/kg<br>The impurity toluene must not exceed 3 g/kg in the technical material.</td><td>1 June 2010</td><td>31 May 2023</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on fluopicolide, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 27 November 2009 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of aquatic organisms,<br>the protection of groundwater when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions,<br>to the risk to operators during application,<br>the potential for long range transport via air.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures and monitoring programmes shall be initiated to verify potential accumulation and exposure in vulnerable areas, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information on the relevance of the metabolite M15 for groundwater by 30 April 2012 at the latest.</td></tr>
<tr><td>298</td><td>Heptamaloxyloglucan<br>CAS No<br>870721-81-6<br>CIPAC No<br>Not available</td><td>Full IUPAC name in footnote (1)<br>Xyl p: xylopyranosyl<br>Glc p: glucopyranosyl<br>Fuc p: fucopyranosyl<br>Gal p: galactopyranosyl<br>Glc-ol: glucitol</td><td>≥ 780 g/kg<br>The impurity Patulin must not exceed 50 μg/kg in the technical material.</td><td>1 June 2010</td><td>31 May 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as plant growth regulator may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on heptamaloxyloglucan, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 27 November 2009 shall be taken into account.</td></tr>
<tr><td>299</td><td>2-phenylphenol (including its salts such as the sodium salt)<br>CAS No 90-43-7<br>CIPAC No 246</td><td>biphenyl-2-ol</td><td>≥ 998 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2010</td><td>31 December 2021</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as a post-harvest fungicide for indoor use may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on 2-phenylphenol, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 27 November 2009, as amended in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2010, shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States must pay particular attention:<br>to the protection of operators and workers and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the application of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>to put in place appropriate waste management practices to handle the waste solution remaining after application, including the cleaning water of the drenching and other application systems. Member States permitting the release of wastewater into the sewage system, shall ensure that a local risk assessment is carried out.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission:<br>further information on the potential for skin depigmentation for workers and consumers due to possible exposure to the metabolite 2-phenylhydroquinone (PHQ) on citrus peel,<br>further information to confirm that the analytical method applied in residue trials correctly quantifies the residues of 2-phenylphenol, PHQ and their conjugates.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 31 December 2011.<br>Furthermore, the Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information to confirm the residue levels occurring as a result of application techniques other than those in drench chambers.<br>They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 31 December 2012.</td></tr>
<tr><td>300</td><td>Malathion<br>CAS No 121-75-5<br>CIPAC No 12</td><td>diethyl (dimethoxyphosphinothioylthio)succinate<br>or<br>S-1,2-bis(ethoxycarbonyl)ethyl O,O-dimethyl phosphorodithioate<br>racemate</td><td>≥ 950 g/kg<br>Impurities:<br>Isomalathion: not more than 2 g/kg</td><td>1 May 2010</td><td>30 April 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as an insecticide in greenhouses with a permanent structure may be authorised. Authorisations shall be limited to professional users.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles, as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on malathion, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on Plants, Animals, Food and Feed shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>((a)) releases from greenhouses, such as condensation water, drain water, soil or artificial substrate, in order to preclude risks to aquatic organisms;<br>((b)) the protection of pollinator colonies purposely placed in the greenhouse;<br>((c)) the protection of operators and workers, so as to ensure that the conditions of use prescribe the use of adequate personal protective equipment, where appropriate;<br>((d)) the protection of consumers in the case of processed commodities.<br>Member States shall ensure that malathion-based formulations are accompanied by the necessary instructions to avoid any risk of formation of isomalathion in excess of the permitted maximum quantities during storage and transport.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures and provide for adequate labelling of plant protection products.</td></tr>
<tr><td>301</td><td>Penoxsulam<br>CAS No 219714-96-2<br>CIPAC No 758</td><td>3-(2,2-difluoroethoxy)-N-(5,8-dimethoxy[1,2,4]triazolo[1,5-c]pyrimidin-2-yl)-α,α,α-trifluorotoluene-2-sulfonamide</td><td>&gt; 980 g/kg<br>The impurity<br>Bis-CHYMP<br>2-chloro-4-[2-(2-chloro-5-methoxy-4-pyrimidinyl)hydrazino]-5-methoxypyrimidine must not exceed 0,1 g/kg in the technical material</td><td>1 August 2010</td><td>31 July 2023</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on penoxsulam, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 22 January 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of aquatic organisms,<br>the dietary exposure of consumers to residues of the metabolite BSCTA in succeeding rotational crops,<br>the protection of groundwater when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the notifier submits to the Commission further information to address the off-field risk to higher aquatic plants. They shall ensure that the notifier provides such information to the Commission by 31 July 2012.<br>The Rapporteur Member State shall inform the Commission in accordance with Article 38 of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009 on the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured.</td></tr>
<tr><td>302</td><td>Proquinazid<br>CAS No 189278-12-4<br>CIPAC No 764</td><td>6-iodo-2-propoxy-3-propylquinazolin-4(3H)-one</td><td>&gt; 950 g/kg</td><td>1 August 2010</td><td>31 July 2022</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on proquinazid, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 22 January 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention:<br>to the long-term risk to earthworm-eating birds for uses in grapevine,<br>to the risk to aquatic organisms,<br>the dietary exposure of consumers to proquinazid residues in products of animal origin and in succeeding rotational crops,<br>to the operator safety.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Rapporteur Member State shall inform the Commission in accordance with Article 38 of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009 on the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured.</td></tr>
<tr><td>303</td><td>Spirodiclofen<br>CAS No 148477-71-8<br>CIPAC No 737</td><td>3-(2,4-dichlorophenyl)-2-oxo-1-oxaspiro[4.5]dec-3-en-4-yl 2,2-dimethylbutyrate</td><td>&gt; 965 g/kg<br>The following impurities must not exceed a certain amount in the technical material:<br>3-(2,4-dichlorophenyl)-4-hydroxy-1-oxaspiro[4.5]dec-3-en-2-one (BAJ-2740 enol): ≤ 6 g/kg<br>N,N-dimethylacetamide: ≤ 4 g/kg</td><td>1 August 2010</td><td>31 July 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as acaricide or insecticide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on spirodiclofen, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 22 January 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention:<br>to the long-term risk to aquatic organisms,<br>to the operator safety,<br>to the risk to bee brood.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>304</td><td>Metalaxyl<br>CAS No 57837-19-1<br>CIPAC No 365</td><td>Methyl N-(methoxyacetyl)-N-(2,6-xylyl)-DL-alaninate</td><td>950 g/kg<br>The impurity 2,6-dimethylaniline was considered of toxicological concern and a maximum level of 1 g/kg is established.</td><td>1 July 2010</td><td>30 June 2023</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on metalaxyl, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 12 March 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>Member States must pay particular attention to the potential contamination of groundwater by the active substance or its degradation products CGA 62826 and CGA 108906 when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions. Risk mitigation measures should be applied where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>305</td><td>Flonicamid (IKI-220)<br>CAS No 158062-67-0<br>CIPAC No 763</td><td>N-cyanomethyl-4-(trifluoromethyl)nicotinamide</td><td>≥ 960 g/kg<br>The impurity toluene must not exceed 3 g/kg in the technical material.</td><td>1 September 2010</td><td>31 August 2023</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on flonicamid, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 22 January 2010, shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the risk to operators and re-entry workers,<br>the risk to bees.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures where appropriate.<br>The Member States shall inform the Commission in accordance with Article 38 of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009 on the specification of the technical material as commercially manufactured.</td></tr>
<tr><td>306</td><td>Triflumizole<br>CAS No 99387-89-0<br>CIPAC No 730</td><td>(E)-4-chloro-α,α,α-trifluoro-N-(1-imidazol-1-yl-2-propoxyethylidene)-o-toluidine</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg<br>Impurities:<br>Toluene: not more than 1 g/kg</td><td>1 July 2010</td><td>30 June 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as fungicide in greenhouses on artificial substrates may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on triflumizole, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 12 March 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>the operator and worker safety: conditions of use shall prescribe the use of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the potential impact on aquatic organisms and must ensure that the conditions of authorisation include, as appropriate, risk mitigation measures.</td></tr>
<tr><td>307</td><td>Sulfuryl fluoride<br>CAS No 002699-79-8<br>CIPAC No 757</td><td>Sulfuryl fluoride</td><td>&gt; 994 g/kg</td><td>1 November 2010</td><td>31 October 2023</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as insecticide/nematicide (fumigant) applied by professional users in sealable structures may be authorised insofar:<br>((a)) these structures are empty; or<br>((b)) where food or feed commodities are present in a fumigated facility, the users and the food business operators ensure that only the food or feed commodities compliant with the existing maximum residue levels for sulfuryl fluoride and fluoride ion set by Regulation (EC) No 396/2005 of the European Parliament and of the Council may enter the food and feed chain; to this purpose, the users and the food business operators shall fully implement measures equivalent to the HACCP principles as laid down in Article 5 of Regulation (EC) No 852/2004 of the European Parliament and of the Council ; in particular, the users shall identify the critical control point at which control is essential to prevent maximum residue levels to be exceeded, and establish and implement effective monitoring procedures at that critical control point.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles, as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on sulfuryl fluoride, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on Plants, Animals, Food and Feed on 7 December 2016 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention to:<br>the risk posed by inorganic fluoride through contaminated products, such as flour and bran that remained in the mill machinery during fumigation, or grain stored in silos in the mill. Measures are required to ensure that only products complying with the existing MRLs enter the food and feed chain;<br>the risk to operators and the risk to workers, such as when re-entering a fumigated structure after aeration. Measures are required to ensure that they wear self-containing breathing apparatus or other appropriate personal protective equipment;<br>the risk to bystanders by applying an appropriate exclusion zone around the fumigated structure.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The notifier shall submit to the Commission, Member States and the Authority monitoring data on tropospheric concentrations of sulfuryl fluoride every fifth year, starting from 30 June 2017 . The limit of detection for the analysis shall be at least 0,5 ppt (equivalent to 2,1 ng sulfuryl fluoride/m <sup> 3 </sup> of tropospheric air).</td></tr>
<tr><td>308</td><td>FEN 560 (also called fenugreek or fenugreek seed powder)<br>CAS No<br>None<br>CIPAC No<br>None<br>The active substance is prepared from the seed powder of Trigonella foenum-graecum L. (fenugreek).</td><td>Not applicable</td><td>100 % fenugreek seed powder without any additive and no extraction; the seed being of human food grade quality.</td><td>1 November 2010</td><td>31 October 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as elicitor of the crop's self-defence mechanisms may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on FEN 560 (fenugreek seed powder), and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 11 May 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment, Member States must pay particular attention to the risk to operators, workers and bystanders.<br>Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures where appropriate.</td></tr>
<tr><td>309</td><td>Haloxyfop-P<br>CAS No Acid: 95977-29-0<br>Ester: 72619-32-0<br>CIPAC No Acid: 526<br>Ester: 526.201</td><td>Acid: (R)-2-[4-(3-chloro-5-trifluoromethyl-2-pyridyloxy)phenoxy]propanoic acid<br>Ester: Methyl (R)-2-{}{4-[3-chloro-5-(trifluoromethyl)-2-pyridyloxy]phenoxy}}propionate</td><td>≥ 940 g/kg<br>(Haloxyfop-P-methyl ester)</td><td>1 January 2011</td><td>31 December 2020</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised at rates not exceeding 0,052 kg active substance per hectare per application, and only one application may be authorised every 3 years.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on haloxyfop-P, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of groundwater from the relevant soil metabolite DE-535 pyridinone when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions,<br>the safety of operators and ensure that conditions of use prescribe the use of adequate personal protective equipment,<br>the protection of aquatic organisms. Conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate, such as adequate buffer zones,<br>the consumer safety as regards the occurrence in groundwater of metabolite DE-535 pyridinol.</td></tr>
<tr><td>310</td><td>Napropamide<br>CAS No 15299-99-7</td><td>(RS)-N,N-diethyl-2-(1-naphthyloxy)propionamide</td><td>≥ 930 g/kg<br>(Racemic mixture)<br>Relevant impurity<br>Toluene: not more than 1,4 g/kg</td><td>1 January 2011</td><td>31 December 2023</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on napropamide, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2010, shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>operator safety: conditions of use shall prescribe the use of adequate personal protective equipment, where necessary,<br>protection of aquatic organisms: conditions of authorisation shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate, such as adequate buffer zones,<br>consumer safety as regards the occurrence in groundwater of the metabolite 2-(1-naphthyloxy)propionic acid, hereinafter ‘NOPA’.<br>The Member States concerned shall ensure that the applicant presents to the Commission, by 31 December 2012 at the latest, information confirming the surface water exposure assessment as regards the photolysis metabolites and the metabolite NOPA and information for the risk assessment of aquatic plants.</td></tr>
<tr><td>311</td><td>Quinmerac<br>CAS No 90717-03-6<br>CIPAC No 563</td><td>7-chloro-3-methylquinoline-8-carboxylic acid</td><td>≥ 980 g/kg</td><td>1 May 2011</td><td>30 April 2024</td><td>PART A<br>Only uses as herbicide may be authorised.<br>PART B<br>For the implementation of the uniform principles as referred to in Article 29(6) of Regulation (EC) No 1107/2009, the conclusions of the review report on quinmerac, and in particular Appendices I and II thereof, as finalised in the Standing Committee on the Food Chain and Animal Health on 28 October 2010 shall be taken into account.<br>In this overall assessment Member States shall pay particular attention to:<br>the protection of groundwater when the active substance is applied in regions with vulnerable soil and/or climatic conditions;<br>the dietary exposure of consumers to residues of quinmerac (and its metabolites) in succeeding rotational crops<br>the risk to aquatic organisms and the long term risk for earthworms.<br>Conditions of use shall include risk mitigation measures, where appropriate.<br>The Member States concerned shall request the submission of information as regards:<br>the potential of plant metabolism to result in an opening of the quinoline ring;<br>residues in rotational crops and the long term risk for earthworms due to the metabolite BH 518-5.<br>They shall ensure that the applicant provides such confirmatory data and information to the Commission by 30 April 2013.</td></tr>
